Your goal is to load and reformat data from a neuroscience paper into a specified structure suitable for downstream analysis.\n    20→- You need to use the **SAME** processing of the data described in the provided reference paper and code repository. \n    21→- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n    22→- We will assess your correctness on **matching the loading and processing described in the reference texts and code**. \n    23→\n    24→## Reference Information\n    25→\n    26→- The reference **paper** \"Separating cognitive and motor processes in the behaving mouse\" is in the file `paper.pdf`.\n    27→- Some parts of the paper that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `methods.txt`. \n    28→- **Code** from the paper are in the directory `code`.\n    29→- **Data** from the paper are in the directory `data`.\n    30→\n    31→## Decoder Task\n    32→\n    33→Temporally align based on **Go cue** onset. \n    34→\n    35→### Decoder Inputs:\n    36→- Time from go cue onset in seconds (continuous, time-varying)\n    37→\n    38→### Decoder Outputs\n    39→- **Lick direction** (left = 0, right = 1, per-trial)\n    40→- **Behavioral context** (WC = 0, DR = 1, per-trial)\n    41→- **Outcome** (incorrect = 0, correct = 1, per-trial)\n    42→- **Tongue velocity**: discretized into two bins with per-session threshold:\n    43→  - 0: < 50th percentile \n    44→  - 1: >= 50th percentile\n    45→- **Paw velocity**: discretized into two bins with per-session threshold:\n    46→  - 0: < 50th percentile \n    47→  - 1: >= 50th percentile\n    48→- **Motion energy**: discretized into two bins with per-session threshold:\n    49→  - 0: < 50th percentile \n    50→  - 1: >= 50th percentile\n    51→\n    52→## Target Data Format\n    53→\n    54→- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `converted_data.pkl`.\n    55→- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n    56→\n    57→```python\n    58→data = {\n    59→    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n    60→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    61→            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n    62→            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n    63→            ...\n    64→        ],\n    65→        [  # list of trials for session 2\n    66→            ...\n    67→        ],\n    68→        ...\n    69→    ],\n    70→\n    71→    'input': [  # list of sessions\n    72→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    73→            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n    74→            ...\n    75→        ],\n    76→        ...\n    77→    ],\n    78→\n    79→    'output': [  # list of sessions\n    80→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    81→            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n    82→            ...\n    83→        ],\n    84→        ...\n    85→    ],\n    86→\n    87→    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    88→    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n    89→\n    90→    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    91→    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n    92→        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    93→    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n    94→\n    95→    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    96→    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    97→    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n    98→        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n    99→        ...\n   100→    ],\n   101→\n   102→    'metadata': {\n   103→        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n   104→        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n   105→        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n   106→        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n   107→        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n   108→        ...\n   109→    }\n   110→}\n   111→```\n   112→\n   113→### Key Format Requirements\n   114→\n   115→- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n   116→  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n   117→  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n   118→  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n   119→\n   120→- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n   121→  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n   122→  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n   123→    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n   124→    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n   125→    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n   126→  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n   127→  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n   128→  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n   129→\n   130→- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n   131→  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n   132→  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n   133→  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n   134→  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n   135→  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n   136→\n   137→- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n   138→- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n   139→  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n   140→\n   141→- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n   142→- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n   143→\n   144→- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n   145→  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n   146→\n   147→- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n   148→  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n   149→- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n   150→  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n   151→\n   152→- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n   153→  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n   154→  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n   155→  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n   156→  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n   157→  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n   158→\n   159→## Python environment\n   160→\n   161→- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n   162→- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n   163→- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n   164→\n   165→## Decoder Reference\n   166→\n   167→- Run this script with `python train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n   168→- Optional arguments:\n   169→  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n   170→  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n   171→  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n   172→- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n   173→\n   174→### Purpose\n   175→\n   176→These functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n   177→1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   178→2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   179→3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   180→4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\n   181→5. (Follow Decoder Task section)\n   322→  - How to discretize continuous outputs (Follow Decoder Task section)\n   323→  - Which inputs/outputs should be **time-varying** vs **per-trial** (Follow Decoder Task section)\n   324→  - How does the reference code load and manipulate this data?\n   325→  - When possible, import or copy code from the reference code in `code` directory. \n   326→- Consult CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4 and reference materials to determine:\n   327→  - Temporal binning for spike rates (if applicable)\n   328→  - Temporal alignment of trials\n   329→  - Data filtering and curation rules for neurons, trials, and sessions\n   330→  - Brain regions recorded from\n   331→- Mapping should be consistent with CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4.\n   332→- Check for variables indicating valid data periods — exclude invalid data\n   333→- Note all decisions, edge cases, and data quality issues\n   334→- Plan and document **sanity and consistency checks** based on dataset size information from the reference texts, code, and data\n   335→\n   336→**Done when**:\n   337→- You have a complete mapping document in CONVERSION_NOTES.md specifying every source-to-target variable mapping under \"Step 5\"\n   338→- You have documented all decisions under \"Step 5\"\n   339→- You have documented sanity and consistency checks under \"Step 5\"\n   340→\n   341→**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 6, update Step 5 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   342→\n   343→---\n   344→\n   345→### Step 6: Script Development\n   346→\n   347→**Goal**: Write the conversion script to `convert_data.py`.\n   348→\n   349→**Actions**:\n   350→- Write all conversion code in the file `convert_data.py`\n   351→- Script should run as **`python -u convert_data.py <outpicklefile>`**\n   352→- Include options:\n   353→  - `--full` (default): Process all sessions\n   354→  - `--sample`: Process only 2 sessions for testing\n   355→  - `--show-processing`: Plot visualizations of EVERY processing step for up to 2 sessions. Save plots as `processing_<session_id>.png`.\n   356→- Reuse code from reference code bases where appropriate\n   357→- Write efficient code:\n   358→  - Vectorize loops\n   359→  - Avoid unnecessary file I/O\n   360→  - Use parallel processing if beneficial\n   361→  - Print timing information to find bottlenecks\n   362→- Plots for `--show-processing` mode should visually convince the user that every step of the conversion is correct.\n   363→  - There are no temporal misalignments\n   364→  - Discretization of continuous outputs is correct\n   365→- Write clear, well-documented functions\n   366→- Handle missing data appropriately (consult references, use sensible defaults, document)\n   367→- Validate data shapes and types at each step\n   368→- Include sanity checks (e.g., trial counts match across arrays)\n   369→\n   370→**Done when**: `convert_data.py` exists and runs efficiently without errors.\n   371→\n   372→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 7 until `convert_data.py` exists and you have updated Step 6 in CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   373→\n   374→---\n   375→\n   376→### Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n   377→\n   378→**Goal**: Validate the conversion on a small sample before processing the full dataset.\n   379→\n   380→**Actions**:\n   381→1. Run `python -u convert_data.py sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing 2>&1 | tee conversion_sample_out.txt`\n   382→  a. Verify the sample data structure matches the specification.\n   383→  b. Check dimensions are consistent across trials/sessions.\n   384→  c. Check that no data is missed during conversion. \n   385→  d. Validate that metadata accurately describes the data.\n   386→2. Improve efficiency of conversion.\n   387→  a. Look at and note timing information for the conversion. \n   388→  b. Estimate how long the full conversion will run. When you do this, make sure that your estimate accounts for differences in lengths/numbers of trials/sessions. \n   389→  c. If full conversion time estimate is longer than 15 minutes, speed up the code by writing more efficient code (vectorizing loops, removing unnecessary or redundant computations) and/or including parallel processing. \n   390→3. Run `python -u train_decoder.py sample_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee verification_sample_out.txt`.\n   391→4. Analyze the output file `verification_sample_out.txt`:\n   392→  a. Verify no errors reported.\n   393→  b. Attempt to address any warnings\n   394→  c. Check input ranges and output value distributions against expectations from reference texts.\n   395→\n   396→**Done when**: \n   397→- `sample_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n   398→- `verification_sample_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n   399→- Processing plots show no anomalies\n   400→- Document sample statistics in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 7\"\n   401→\n   402→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 8 until `sample_data.pkl` and `verification_sample_out.txt` files exist, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 7 is filled in. \n   403→\n   404→---\n   405→\n   406→### Step 8: Sample Decoder Training\n   407→\n   408→**Goal**: Verify the decoder runs successfully on sample data.\n   409→\n   410→**Actions**:\n   411→1. Run `python -u train_decoder.py sample_data.pkl 2>&1 | tee train_decoder_sample_out.txt`\n   412→2. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n   413→3. **Check accuracy**: Verify accuracy is above chance for EVERY output\n   414→\n   415→**Investigate any issues**:\n   416→- If accuracy is low: check for conversion bugs or reconsider output representation\n   417→\n   418→**Done when**: \n   419→- Decoder training completes without errors\n   420→- Loss decreases over epochs\n   421→- Accuracies are above chance\n   422→- Document all results in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 8\"\n   423→\n   424→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 9 until `train_decoder_sample_out.txt` file exists, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 8 is filled in. \n   425→\n   426→---\n   427→\n   428→### Step 9: Full Conversion and Validation\n   429→\n   430→**Goal**: Process the complete dataset.\n   431→\n   432→**Actions**:\n   433→1. Review your time estimate from Step 7.\n   434→2. If estimated time is long (e.g. longer than 15 minutes), optimize bottlenecks first.\n   435→3. Run `python -u convert_data.py converted_data.pkl --full 2>&1 | tee conversion_full_out.txt`\n   436→4. As the code runs, update your estimates of how long processing will take. If it is much longer than your previous estimate (> 1.5x), kill the process, optimize bottlenecks, and repeat. \n   437→5. Run `python -u train_decoder.py converted_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee verification_full_out.txt`. \n   438→6. Check that no data was lost during conversion \n   439→7. Spot-check a few sessions to verify data integrity\n   440→8. **Check for consistency** between dataset statistics in `verification_full_out.txt` and the reference texts\n   441→9. Investigate any inconsistencies, and revise the conversion script until all dataset statistics are consistent\n   442→\n   443→**Done when**:\n   444→- `converted_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n   445→- `verification_full_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n   446→- **ALL** dataset statistics are consistent with values from reference texts\n   447→- You have documented statistics and consistency in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 9\"\n   448→\n   449→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 10 until `converted_data.pkl` and `verification_full_out.txt` files exist and CONVERSION_NOTES.MD Step 9 is filled in.\n   450→\n   451→---\n   452→\n   453→### Step 10: Critical Review 1\n   454→\n   455→**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain.\n   456→\n   457→**Check 1: Output log verification**\n   458→Read through `verification_full_out.txt` to find and fix **all errors**. Address each warning by either fixing its cause or verifying it that it is not possible to fix it. Explain why each warning could not be fixed in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   459→\n   460→**Check 2: Construct sanity checks**\n   461→- Develop at least one sanity check each on neural, input, and output data.\n   462→- These must involve loading in the original data files (NOT through your conversion code).\n   463→- Construct comparison tests and criteria using `np.allclose()`. \n   464→- These tests can be spot-checks of the data, e.g. neural activity at trial 5, timepoint 10, neuron 3. \n   465→- Fix all mismatches/sanity check failures. \n   466→- Document your sanity checks to `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   467→\n   468→**Check 3: Reference code comparison**\n   469→- Compare your code to the reference code and methods. \n   470→- For each major processing step — (a) data loading, (b) neuron/trial filtering, (c) temporal alignment, (d) binning, (e) input construction, (f) output construction — identify the corresponding code in your script and in the reference, and compare the logic. \n   471→- Check whether you use the same variables, filtering, and processing as the reference code. \n   472→- Fix any mismatches. \n   473→- Document your comparisons in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   474→- Explain your reasoning for any differences in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`.\n   475→\n   476→**Check 4: Key statistics comparison**\n   477→- Verify that key statistics (number of animals, sessions, trials, neurons, input and output ranges, output distributions) match information available in the reference paper. \n   478→- Write code to investigate any discrepancies. \n   479→- Fix any discovered issues. \n   480→- Document your comparisons in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   481→\n   482→**Check 5: Check for edge cases**\n   483→- Check for off-by-one errors at the beginning/ends of trials, sessions, and subjects, or in definitions of the inputs and outputs. \n   484→- There may be minor issues in the data that your code must handle. Check that your code robustly handles issues in the original data. \n   485→\n   486→**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n   487→1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n   488→2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n   489→3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n   490→4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n   491→\n   492→Write a report to CONVERSION_NOTES.md describing **every** check you did, to help convince the user that the conversion code works. **Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 10\".\n   493→\n   494→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 11 until you have checked for consistency between **every** statistic in the reference texts, code, and data and documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Step 10. \n   495→\n   496→---\n   497→\n   498→### Step 11: Full Decoder Training\n   499→\n   500→**Goal**: Validate the decoder on the **complete** dataset.\n   501→\n   502→**⚠️ IMPORTANT**: This step must complete fully. Do not skip or abbreviate. Do not proceed to Step 12 until this step is completely finished.\n   503→\n   504→**Actions**:\n   505→1. Run `python -u train_decoder.py converted_data.pkl --plot-samples 2>&1 | tee train_decoder_full_out.txt`. If the GPU does not have sufficient RAM for the network training, use the flag `--cpu` to specify to use the CPU to train. \n   506→2. **Wait for complete execution** — this may take significant time for large datasets\n   507→3. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n   508→4. **Check accuracy**: High accuracy is an indicator that data conversion has been done correctly. Accuracy near chance is a sign that there might be issues in temporal alignment of signals, choice of data streams, or filtering or processing of data. Compare accuracy to expectations based on the paper. \n   509→\n   510→**Done when**: \n   511→1. Full decoder training completes (the script finishes running)\n   512→2. Accuracy results are documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 11\" with a table of accuracies\n   513→\n   514→---\n   515→\n   516→### Step 12: Critical Review 2\n   517→\n   518→**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain. Perform all of the following checks:\n   519→\n   520→**Check 1: Accuracy vs chance analysis**\n   521→- For each output, compare validation accuracy to chance (1/num_classes)\n   522→- Accuracy below chance indicates a bug, investigate and fix conversion issues to improve this. \n   523→- Accuracy below 1.5x chance may also indicate a bug, investigate whether changes in conversion logic could improve accuracy. \n   524→\n   525→**Check 2: Accuracy comparison to paper**\n   526→- Find every decoding accuracy reported in the reference paper\n   527→- Create a table comparing your accuracy to the paper's accuracy for each variable\n   528→- If your accuracy is lower than the reported accuracy, this may indicate a bug in your conversion that you must fix. \n   529→- Differences in algorithm/architecture should be relatively small because of the data dimensionality and size. Do not use this as an excuse to dismiss differences, investigate them completely before moving on. \n   530→\n   531→**Check 3: Train vs validation gap**\n   532→- If training accuracy is much higher (>1.5x) than validation accuracy for any output, investigate overfitting or data leakage. \n   533→\n   534→**If accuracy is low for an output, try these specific debugging steps:**\n   535→1. Verify the output values are correct by loading raw data and checking 3 specific trials\n   536→2. Check temporal alignment — plot neural + output for a single trial to verify they're synchronized\n   537→3. Check whether the output has enough variation (not 99% one class)\n   538→4. Check that filtering steps for neural activity streams are followed. \n   539→5. Check that processing of the data matches the reference code and paper. \n   540→\n   541→**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n   542→1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n   543→2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n   544→3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n   545→4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n   546→\n   547→**Report all checks and results** to `CONVERSION_NOTES.md` to convince the user that the conversion and decoding has been done correctly. \n   548→\n   549→**Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 12\".\n   550→\n   551→---\n   552→\n   553→### Step 13: Documentation and Cleanup\n   554→\n   555→**Goal**: Finalize documentation and organize files.\n   556→\n   557→**Actions**:\n   558→- Ensure CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete with all:\n   559→  - Key decisions and rationale\n   560→  - Checks for correctness of the conversion\n   561→  - Validation results and tables\n   562→  - Any issues found and how they were resolved\n   563→- Create a user-facing `README.md` summarizing:\n   564→  - Dataset description\n   565→  - How to load and use the converted data\n   566→  - Output format specification\n   567→  - Key statistics\n   568→- Move analysis/investigation scripts to a `cache/` folder\n   569→- Create `cache/README_CACHE.md` documenting cached files\n   570→\n   571→**Done when**: README.md exists, CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete, and directory is clean.\n   572→\n   573→---\n   574→\n   575→## CONVERSION_NOTES.md Template\n   576→\n   577→**Create this file IMMEDIATELY in Step 0. data\n   829→- **Trial alignment**: Align trials to meaningful events (stimulus onset, movement, etc.)\n   830→- **Time windows**: Choose appropriate pre/post-event windows\n   831→- **Neuron curation**: Choose filtering criteria for the quality of each neuron's recording based on reference texts\n   832→- **Trial curation**: Choose quality filtering criteria for trials based on reference texts or bad or missing data\n   833→\n   834→### Computational Efficiency\n   835→- Be mindful of memory usage with large datasets\n   836→- Use appropriate data types (float32 vs. float64)\n   837→- Consider lazy loading for very large datasets\n   838→- Implement efficient array operations (vectorization)\n   839→\n   840→## Success Criteria\n   841→\n   842→A successful conversion should:\n   843→1. Match the target data structure exactly\n   844→2. Preserve all relevant information from the source\n   845→3. Have consistent dimensions across trials/sessions\n   846→4. Do this BEFORE exploring any code or data. Document everything in this file as you work.\n\n2. **FOLLOW STEPS IN ORDER**: Do not skip steps. Do not proceed to step N+1 until step N is complete. You will be assessed on this consistency.\n\n---\n\n## Project Context\n\n- You are a computational neuroscientist. Your goal is to load and reformat data from a neuroscience paper into a specified structure suitable for downstream analysis.\n- You need to use the **SAME** processing of the data described in the provided reference paper and code repository. \n- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n- We will assess your correctness on **matching the loading and processing described in the reference texts and code**. \n\n## Reference Information\n\n- The reference **paper** \"Separating cognitive and motor processes in the behaving mouse\" is in the file `paper.pdf`.\n- Some parts of the paper that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `methods.txt`. \n- **Code** from the paper are in the directory `code`.\n- **Data** from the paper are in the directory `data`.\n\n## Decoder Task\n\nTemporally align based on **Go cue** onset. \n\n### Decoder Inputs:\n- Time from go cue onset in seconds (continuous, time-varying)\n\n### Decoder Outputs\n- **Lick direction** (left = 0, right = 1, per-trial)\n- **Behavioral context** (WC = 0, DR = 1, per-trial)\n- **Outcome** (incorrect = 0, correct = 1, per-trial)\n- **Tongue velocity**: discretized into two bins with per-session threshold:\n  - 0: < 50th percentile \n  - 1: >= 50th percentile\n- **Paw velocity**: discretized into two bins with per-session threshold:\n  - 0: < 50th percentile \n  - 1: >= 50th percentile\n- **Motion energy**: discretized into two bins with per-session threshold:\n  - 0: < 50th percentile \n  - 1: >= 50th percentile\n\n## Target Data Format\n\n- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `converted_data.pkl`.\n- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n\n```python\ndata = {\n    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        [  # list of trials for session 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'input': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'output': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n\n    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n\n    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'metadata': {\n        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        ...\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Key Format Requirements\n\n- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n\n- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n\n- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n\n- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n\n- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n\n- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n\n## Python environment\n\n- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n\n## Decoder Reference\n\n- Run this script with `python train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n- Optional arguments:\n  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n\n### Purpose\n\nThese functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n3. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\n\n### Consistency requirements\n\nYour processing and formatting must **match the reference paper and code** with respect to:\n- Loading of data\n- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\n- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\n- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\n\nTo check consistency, you must compare statistics available in the reference paper and your converted dataset. \n\nWhenever possible, invent **SANITY CHECKS** that your loading and processing matches the reference paper and code. \n\nDiscrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above. \n\nCarefully verify your work after every step. Skip probes with invalid clu data (not a group or missing 'tm')\n   310→        if not isinstance(clu_group, h5py.Group) or 'tm' not in clu_group:\n   311→            clu_list.append([])  # empty unit list for this probe\n   312→            continue\n   313→        \n   314→        n_units = clu_group['tm'].shape[0]\n   315→        units = []\n   316→        for u in range(n_units):\n   317→            unit = {}\n   318→            unit['tm'] = np.array(f[clu_group['tm'][u, 0]]).flatten()\n   319→            unit['trial'] = np.array(f[clu_group['trial'][u, 0]]).flatten().astype(int)\n   320→            unit['trialtm'] = np.array(f[clu_group['trialtm'][u, 0]]).flatten()\n   321→            unit['quality'] = read_h5_string(f, clu_group['quality'][u, 0])\n   322→            units.append(unit)\n   323→        clu_list.append(units)\n   324→    session['clu'] = clu_list\n   325→    \n   326→    # ---- SpikeGLX metadata (for video offset) ----\n   327→    try:\n   328→        sglx_fs = float(f['obj/sglx/fs'][0, 0])\n   329→        bitcode_bitstart = np.array(f['obj/sglx/bitcode/bitstart']).flatten()\n   330→        session['sglx'] = {'fs': sglx_fs, 'bitcode_bitstart': bitcode_bitstart}\n   331→    except:\n   332→        session['sglx'] = None\n   333→    \n   334→    # ---- Trajectory data ----\n   335→    traj_data = []\n   336→    n_cams = f['obj/traj'].shape[0]\n   337→    for cam in range(n_cams):\n   338→        traj_ref = f['obj/traj'][cam, 0]\n   339→        traj_group = f[traj_ref]\n   340→        \n   341→        cam_data = {\n   342→            'n_trials': traj_group['ts'].shape[0],\n   343→            'trials': []\n   344→        }\n   345→        \n   346→        # Get feature names from first trial\n   347→        try:\n   348→            fn_ref = traj_group['featNames'][0, 0]\n   349→            fn_data = f[fn_ref]\n   350→            feat_names = []\n   351→            if fn_data.dtype == np.object_:\n   352→                for i in range(fn_data.shape[1] if len(fn_data.shape) > 1 else fn_data.shape[0]):\n   353→                    idx = (0, i) if len(fn_data.shape) > 1 else (i,)\n   354→                    name = read_h5_string(f, fn_data[idx])\n   355→                    feat_names.append(name)\n   356→            cam_data['feat_names'] = feat_names\n   357→        except:\n   358→            cam_data['feat_names'] = []\n   359→        \n   360→        # Load per-trial data\n   361→        for trix in range(cam_data['n_trials']):\n   362→            trial_data = {}\n   363→            try:\n   364→                ts_ref = traj_group['ts'][trix, 0]\n   365→                ts = np.array(f[ts_ref])  # shape: (n_feats, 3, n_timepoints) in h5py\n   366→                trial_data['ts'] = ts  # Keep in h5py order\n   367→            except:\n   368→                trial_data['ts'] = None\n   369→            \n   370→            try:\n   371→                ft_ref = traj_group['frameTimes'][trix, 0]\n   372→                ft = np.array(f[ft_ref]).flatten()\n   373→                trial_data['frameTimes'] = ft\n   374→            except:\n   375→                trial_data['frameTimes'] = None\n   376→            \n   377→            try:\n   378→                nd_ref = traj_group['NdroppedFrames'][trix, 0]\n   379→                nd = np.array(f[nd_ref]).flatten()\n   380→                trial_data['NdroppedFrames'] = nd[0] if len(nd) > 0 else np.nan\n   381→            except:\n   382→                trial_data['NdroppedFrames'] = np.nan\n   383→            \n   384→            cam_data['trials'].append(trial_data)\n   385→        \n   386→        traj_data.append(cam_data)\n   387→    \n   388→    session['traj'] = traj_data\n   389→    \n   390→    f.close()\n   391→    return session\n   392→\n   393→\n   394→def load_motion_energy(me_filepath):\n   395→    \"\"\"Load motion energy from .mat file, handling multiple format variants.\"\"\"\n   396→    try:\n   397→        data = sio.loadmat(me_filepath, squeeze_me=False)\n   398→        me_raw = data['me']\n   399→        \n   400→        # Determine format\n   401→        if me_raw.dtype.names and 'data' in me_raw.dtype.names:\n   402→            # Struct format: me.data, me.moveThresh\n   403→            me_data_field = me_raw['data'][0, 0]\n   404→            \n   405→            # Check if me.data is itself a struct (double-nested)\n   406→            # MATLAB code: if isstruct(me.data); me.data = me.data.data; end\n   407→            if hasattr(me_data_field, 'dtype') and me_data_field.dtype.names and 'data' in me_data_field.dtype.names:\n   408→                # Double-nested: me.data.data contains the actual trial data\n   409→                me_data_arr = me_data_field['data'][0, 0]\n   410→                try:\n   411→                    me_thresh_inner = me_data_field['moveThresh'][0, 0]\n   412→                    me_thresh = float(me_thresh_inner.flatten()[0])\n   413→                except:\n   414→                    me_thresh = float(me_raw['moveThresh'][0, 0].flatten()[0])\n   415→            else:\n   416→                # Normal struct: me.data is the trial data\n   417→                me_data_arr = me_data_field\n   418→                me_thresh = float(me_raw['moveThresh'][0, 0].flatten()[0])\n   419→        else:\n   420→            # Direct format: me is the trial data array directly\n   421→            me_data_arr = me_raw\n   422→            me_thresh = 0  # No threshold available\n   423→        \n   424→        # Extract per-trial data\n   425→        me_trials = []\n   426→        for i in range(me_data_arr.shape[0]):\n   427→            trial_me = me_data_arr[i, 0]\n   428→            if trial_me is not None and hasattr(trial_me, 'size') and trial_me.size > 0:\n   429→                me_trials.append(trial_me.flatten())\n   430→            else:\n   431→                me_trials.append(np.array([]))\n   432→        \n   433→        return {'data': me_trials, 'moveThresh': me_thresh}\n   434→    except Exception as e:\n   435→        print(f'  Warning: Could not load motion energy: {e}')\n   436→        return None\n   437→\n   438→\n   439→def compute_video_offset(session):\n   440→    \"\"\"Compute video-neural offset matching findVideoOffset.m.\"\"\"\n   441→    if session['sglx'] is None:\n   442→        return 0.0\n   443→    \n   444→    bitStart_mode = float(np.median(session['bp']['ev']['bitStart'][~np.isnan(session['bp']['ev']['bitStart'])]))\n   445→    # Use scipy.stats.mode equivalent\n   446→    from scipy import stats\n   447→    bitStart_vals = session['bp']['ev']['bitStart'][~np.isnan(session['bp']['ev']['bitStart'])]\n   448→    if len(bitStart_vals) > 0:\n   449→        bitStart_mode = float(stats.mode(bitStart_vals, keepdims=False).mode)\n   450→    else:\n   451→        bitStart_mode = 0.0\n   452→    \n   453→    bitcode_bitstart_vals = session['sglx']['bitcode_bitstart'][~np.isnan(session['sglx']['bitcode_bitstart'])]\n   454→    if len(bitcode_bitstart_vals) > 0:\n   455→        vidFileOffset = float(stats.mode(bitcode_bitstart_vals, keepdims=False).mode) / session['sglx']['fs']\n   456→    else:\n   457→        vidFileOffset = 0.0\n   458→    \n   459→    vidshift = vidFileOffset - bitStart_mode\n   460→    return vidshift\n   461→\n   462→\n   463→def process_spikes(session, probe_idx, time_axis):\n   464→    \"\"\"Process spikes: align to goCue, bin, smooth, filter.\n   465→    Returns: firing_rates (n_neurons, n_timepoints, n_trials), cluid_mask\n   466→    \"\"\"\n   467→    units = session['clu'][probe_idx]\n   468→    goCue = session['bp']['ev']['goCue']\n   469→    Ntrials = session['bp']['Ntrials']\n   470→    \n   471→    edges = np.append(time_axis, time_axis[-1] + DT)\n   472→    n_time = len(time_axis)\n   473→    \n   474→    # Filter out garbage clusters\n   475→    valid_units = []\n   476→    for u_idx, unit in enumerate(units):\n   477→        quality = unit['quality'].lower().strip()\n   478→        if 'garbage' not in quality:\n   479→            valid_units.append(u_idx)\n   480→    \n   481→    if len(valid_units) == 0:\n   482→        return None, []\n   483→    \n   484→    # Compute firing rates for valid units\n   485→    firing_rates = np.zeros((len(valid_units), n_time, Ntrials), dtype=np.float32)\n   486→    \n   487→    for i, u_idx in enumerate(valid_units):\n   488→        unit = units[u_idx]\n   489→        trial_nums = unit['trial']\n   490→        trialtm = unit['trialtm']\n   491→        \n   492→        for j in range(Ntrials):\n   493→            trial_num = j + 1  # MATLAB 1-indexed\n   494→            spk_mask = trial_nums == trial_num\n   495→            \n   496→            if not np.any(spk_mask):\n   497→                continue\n   498→            \n   499→            # Align to goCue\n   500→            aligned_times = trialtm[spk_mask] - goCue[j]\n   501→            \n   502→            # Bin spikes\n   503→            counts, _ = np.histogram(aligned_times, bins=edges)\n   504→            \n   505→            # Convert to firing rate and smooth\n   506→            fr = counts.astype(np.float64) / DT\n   507→            fr_smooth = smooth_signal(fr, SMOOTH_N)\n   508→            firing_rates[i, :, j] = fr_smooth\n   509→    \n   510→    # Compute mean firing rate across all trials for each unit (for filtering)\n   511→    # Match removeLowFRClusters: mean of PSTH across conditions\n   512→    mean_frs = np.mean(np.mean(firing_rates, axis=2), axis=1)  # mean over time and trials\n   513→    \n   514→    # Filter low FR units\n   515→    fr_mask = mean_frs > LOW_FR\n   516→    \n   517→    if np.sum(fr_mask) == 0:\n   518→        return None, []\n   519→    \n   520→    firing_rates = firing_rates[fr_mask]\n   521→    kept_units = [valid_units[i] for i in range(len(valid_units)) if fr_mask[i]]\n   522→    \n   523→    return firing_rates, kept_units\n   524→\n   525→\n   526→def extract_feature_velocity(session, cam_idx, feat_name, time_axis, vidshift):\n   527→    \"\"\"Extract velocity for a DLC feature, matching findPosition + findVelocity.\n   528→    Returns: velocity magnitude (n_timepoints, n_trials)\n   529→    \"\"\"\n   530→    Ntrials = session['bp']['Ntrials']\n   531→    goCue = session['bp']['ev']['goCue']\n   532→    \n   533→    cam_data = session['traj'][cam_idx]\n   534→    feat_names = cam_data['feat_names']\n   535→    \n   536→    # Find feature index\n   537→    feat_idx = None\n   538→    for i, name in enumerate(feat_names):\n   539→        if name.lower() == feat_name.lower():\n   540→            feat_idx = i\n   541→            break\n   542→    \n   543→    if feat_idx is None:\n   544→        return np.full((len(time_axis), Ntrials), np.nan)\n   545→    \n   546→    is_tongue = 'tongue' in feat_name.lower()\n   547→    \n   548→    xpos = np.full((len(time_axis), Ntrials), np.nan)\n   549→    ypos = np.full((len(time_axis), Ntrials), np.nan)\n   550→    \n   551→    for trix in range(Ntrials):\n   552→        trial = cam_data['trials'][trix]\n   553→        \n   554→        if trial['ts'] is None:\n   555→            continue\n   556→        if np.isnan(trial['NdroppedFrames']):\n   557→            continue\n   558→        \n   559→        ts = trial['ts']  # (n_feats, 3, n_timepoints) in h5py\n   560→        \n   561→        # Get frame times\n   562→        if trial['frameTimes'] is not None and not np.all(np.isnan(trial['frameTimes'])):\n   563→            frame_times = trial['frameTimes']\n   564→        else:\n   565→            n_frames = ts.shape[2]\n   566→            frame_times = np.arange(1, n_frames + 1) / VIDEO_FS\n   567→        \n   568→        # Get x, y for this feature\n   569→        x = ts[feat_idx, 0, :]  # x coordinate\n   570→        y = ts[feat_idx, 1, :]  # y coordinate\n   571→        \n   572→        # Align time to goCue\n   573→        aligned_times = frame_times - vidshift - goCue[trix]\n   574→        \n   575→        # Smooth non-tongue features (mySmooth with N=1 and 'reflect')\n   576→        # In the reference code, non-tongue features are smoothed with mySmooth(ts, 1, 'reflect')\n   577→        # N=1 means no smoothing\n   578→        \n   579→        # Interpolate to time axis\n   580→        try:\n   581→            valid = ~np.isnan(x) & ~np.isnan(aligned_times)\n   582→            if np.sum(valid) < 2:\n   583→                continue\n   584→            \n   585→            f_x = interp1d(aligned_times[valid], x[valid], kind='linear',\n   586→                          bounds_error=False, fill_value=np.nan)\n   587→            f_y = interp1d(aligned_times[valid], y[valid], kind='linear',\n   588→                          bounds_error=False, fill_value=np.nan)\n   589→            \n   590→            xpos[:, trix] = f_x(time_axis)\n   591→            ypos[:, trix] = f_y(time_axis)\n   592→        except:\n   593→            continue\n   594→        \n   595→        # Fill missing for non-tongue\n   596→        if not is_tongue:\n   597→            mask_nan = np.isnan(xpos[:, trix])\n   598→            if np.any(mask_nan) and not np.all(mask_nan):\n   599→                xpos[:, trix] = _fill_nearest(xpos[:, trix])\n   600→                ypos[:, trix] = _fill_nearest(ypos[:, trix])\n   601→    \n   602→    # Compute velocity (gradient, matching findVelocity.m)\n   603→    xvel = np.full_like(xpos, np.nan)\n   604→    yvel = np.full_like(ypos, np.nan)\n   605→    \n   606→    for trix in range(Ntrials):\n   607→        if np.all(np.isnan(xpos[:, trix])):\n   608→            continue\n   609→        \n   610→        xv = np.gradient(xpos[:, trix])\n   611→        yv = np.gradient(ypos[:, trix])\n   612→        \n   613→        if not is_tongue:\n   614→            # Subtract baseline derivative\n   615→            base_x = np.nanmedian(np.diff(xpos[:, trix]))\n   616→            base_y = np.nanmedian(np.diff(ypos[:, trix]))\n   617→            xv = xv - base_x\n   618→            yv = yv - base_y\n   619→            # Fill missing\n   620→            xv = _fill_nearest(xv)\n   621→            yv = _fill_nearest(yv)\n   622→        else:\n   623→            # Set tongue velocity to 0 if not visible\n   624→            xv[np.isnan(xv)] = 0\n   625→            yv[np.isnan(yv)] = 0\n   626→        \n   627→        xvel[:, trix] = xv\n   628→        yvel[:, trix] = yv\n   629→    \n   630→    # Compute velocity magnitude\n   631→    vel_mag = np.sqrt(xvel**2 + yvel**2)\n   632→    \n   633→    return vel_mag\n   634→\n   635→\n   636→def _fill_nearest(arr):\n   637→    \"\"\"Fill NaN values with nearest non-NaN value.\"\"\"\n   638→    mask = np.isnan(arr)\n   639→    if not np.any(mask) or np.all(mask):\n   640→        return arr\n   641→    arr = arr.copy()\n   642→    # Forward fill then backward fill\n   643→    valid = np.where(~mask)[0]\n   644→    if len(valid) == 0:\n   645→        return arr\n   646→    for i in range(len(arr)):\n   647→        if mask[i]:\n   648→            # Find nearest valid\n   649→            dists = np.abs(valid - i)\n   650→            arr[i] = arr[valid[np.argmin(dists)]]\n   651→    return arr\n   652→\n   653→\n   654→def align_motion_energy(me_data, session, time_axis, vidshift):\n   655→    \"\"\"Align motion energy to goCue, matching loadMotionEnergy.m.\"\"\"\n   656→    Ntrials = session['bp']['Ntrials']\n   657→    goCue = session['bp']['ev']['goCue']\n   658→    \n   659→    me_aligned = np.full((len(time_axis), Ntrials), np.nan)\n   660→    \n   661→    n_me_trials = len(me_data['data'])\n   662→    \n   663→    for trix in range(min(Ntrials, n_me_trials)):\n   664→        trial_me = me_data['data'][trix]\n   665→        if trial_me.size == 0:\n   666→            continue\n   667→        \n   668→        # Get frame times for this trial\n   669→        cam_data = session['traj'][0]  # camera 0\n   670→        trial_traj = cam_data['trials'][trix] if trix < len(cam_data['trials']) else None\n   671→        \n   672→        if trial_traj is not None and trial_traj['frameTimes'] is not None and not np.all(np.isnan(trial_traj['frameTimes'])):\n   673→            frame_times = trial_traj['frameTimes']\n   674→            aligned_times = frame_times - vidshift - goCue[trix]\n   675→        else:\n   676→            # Fallback: create frame times\n   677→            n_frames = trial_me.shape[0]\n   678→            if trial_traj is not None and trial_traj['ts'] is not None:\n   679→                n_frames = trial_traj['ts'].shape[2]\n   680→            frame_times = np.arange(1, n_frames + 1) / VIDEO_FS\n   681→            aligned_times = frame_times - 0.5 - goCue[trix]\n   682→        \n   683→        # Trim to same length\n   684→        n = min(len(trial_me), len(aligned_times))\n   685→        if n < 2:\n   686→            continue\n   687→        \n   688→        try:\n   689→            f_me = interp1d(aligned_times[:n], trial_me[:n], kind='linear',\n   690→                           bounds_error=False, fill_value=np.nan)\n   691→            me_aligned[:, trix] = f_me(time_axis)\n   692→        except:\n   693→            continue\n   694→    \n   695→    # Fill NaN with nearest\n   696→    for trix in range(Ntrials):\n   697→        if not np.all(np.isnan(me_aligned[:, trix])):\n   698→            me_aligned[:, trix] = _fill_nearest(me_aligned[:, trix])\n   699→    \n   700→    return me_aligned\n   701→\n   702→\n   703→def get_available_sessions():\n   704→    \"\"\"Get list of all available sessions with their metadata.\"\"\"\n   705→    sessions = []\n   706→    \n   707→    # Parse loading scripts to get probe info\n   708→    script_dir = 'code/DataLoadingScripts/Recording and video'\n   709→    probe_map = {}  # (animal, date) -> probes\n   710→    \n   711→    for f in sorted(os.listdir(script_dir)):\n   712→        if f.endswith('.m'):\n   713→            with open(os.path.join(script_dir, f)) as fh:\n   714→                content = fh.read()\n   715→            anm = None\n   716→            date = None\n   717→            for line in content.split('\\n'):\n   718→                line = line.strip()\n   719→                if line.startswith('%'):\n   720→                    continue\n   721→                m = re.search(r\"meta\\(end(?:\\+1)?\\)\\.anm\\s*=\\s*'(\\w+)'\", line)\n   722→                if m:\n   723→                    anm = m.group(1)\n   724→                m = re.search(r\"meta\\(end(?:\\+1)?\\)\\.date\\s*=\\s*'([\\d-]+)'\", line)\n   725→                if m:\n   726→                    date = m.group(1)\n   727→                m = re.search(r\"meta\\(end(?:\\+1)?\\)\\.probe\\s*=\\s*(.+?);\", line)\n   728→                if m:\n   729→                    probe_str = m.group(1).strip()\n   730→                    if '[' in probe_str:\n   731→                        probes = [int(x) for x in re.findall(r'\\d+', probe_str)]\n   732→                    else:\n   733→                        probes = [int(probe_str)]\n   734→                    if anm and date:\n   735→                        probe_map[(anm, date)] = probes\n   736→    \n   737→    # Find all data files\n   738→    for data_dir in ['data/Ephys_Behavior', 'data/RandomizedDelay_Ephys_Behavior']:\n   739→        if not os.path.exists(data_dir):\n   740→            continue\n   741→        for fn in sorted(os.listdir(data_dir)):\n   742→            if not fn.startswith('data_structure_'):\n   743→                continue\n   744→            m = re.match(r'data_structure_(\\w+)_(\\d{4}-\\d{2}-\\d{2})\\.mat', fn)\n   745→            if not m:\n   746→                continue\n   747→            \n   748→            animal = m.group(1)\n   749→            date = m.group(2)\n   750→            \n   751→            # Skip behavior-only sessions (MAH animals)\n   752→            if animal.startswith('MAH'):\n   753→                continue\n   754→            \n   755→            filepath = os.path.join(data_dir, fn)\n   756→            \n   757→            # Get probe info from loading scripts\n   758→            probes = probe_map.get((animal, date), [1])  # default to probe 1\n   759→            \n   760→            # Check for motion energy file\n   761→            me_fn = fn.replace('data_structure_', 'motionEnergy_')\n   762→            me_filepath = os.path.join(data_dir, me_fn)\n   763→            has_me = os.path.exists(me_filepath)\n   764→            \n   765→            sessions.append({\n   766→                'animal': animal,\n   767→                'date': date,\n   768→                'filepath': filepath,\n   769→                'probes': probes,\n   770→                'me_filepath': me_filepath if has_me else None,\n   771→                'data_dir': data_dir\n   772→            })\n   773→    \n   774→    return sessions\n   775→\n   776→\n   777→def check_session_inclusion(session_data, probes):\n   778→    \"\"\"Check if session meets inclusion criteria.\n   779→    Matching UseInclusionCritera.m: >40 R hit DR trials AND >40 L hit DR trials.\n   780→    \"\"\"\n   781→    bp = session_data['bp']\n   782→    \n   783→    # Count R hit DR trials (no stim, no autowater, no early)\n   784→    r_hit_dr = bp['R'] & bp['hit'] & ~bp['stim_enable'] & ~bp['autowater'] & ~bp['early']\n   785→    l_hit_dr = bp['L'] & bp['hit'] & ~bp['stim_enable'] & ~bp['autowater'] & ~bp['early']\n   786→    \n   787→    n_r = np.sum(r_hit_dr)\n   788→    n_l = np.sum(l_hit_dr)\n   789→    \n   790→    return n_r > MIN_HIT_TRIALS and n_l > MIN_HIT_TRIALS, n_r, n_l\n   791→\n   792→\n   793→def process_session(sess_info, time_axis, show_processing=False):\n   794→    \"\"\"Process a single session and return formatted data.\"\"\"\n   795→    animal = sess_info['animal']\n   796→    date = sess_info['date']\n   797→    probes = sess_info['probes']\n   798→    \n   799→    print(f'  Loading {animal}_{date}...')\n   800→    t0 = time.time()\n   801→    \n   802→    session = load_session_data(sess_info['filepath'])\n   803→    t_load = time.time() - t0\n   804→    print(f'    Loaded in {t_load:.1f}s')\n   805→    \n   806→    # Check inclusion criteria\n   807→    included, n_r, n_l = check_session_inclusion(session, probes)\n   808→    if not included:\n   809→        print(f'    EXCLUDED: R hit DR={n_r}, L hit DR={n_l} (need >{MIN_HIT_TRIALS} each)')\n   810→        return None\n   811→    print(f'    R hit DR={n_r}, L hit DR={n_l}')\n   812→    \n   813→    # Process spikes for each probe and combine\n   814→    t0 = time.time()\n   815→    all_firing_rates = []\n   816→    for p in probes:\n   817→        p_idx = p - 1  # Convert to 0-indexed\n   818→        if p_idx >= len(session['clu']):\n   819→            print(f'    Warning: probe {p} not found, skipping')\n   820→            continue\n   821→        fr, kept = process_spikes(session, p_idx, time_axis)\n   822→        if fr is not None and len(kept) > 0:\n   823→            all_firing_rates.append(fr)\n   824→            print(f'    Probe {p}: {fr.shape[0]} units kept')\n   825→    \n   826→    if len(all_firing_rates) == 0:\n   827→        print(f'    EXCLUDED: no valid units')\n   828→        return None\n   829→    \n   830→    # Concatenate across probes\n   831→    firing_rates = np.concatenate(all_firing_rates, axis=0)  # (n_neurons, n_time, n_trials)\n   832→    n_neurons = firing_rates.shape[0]\n   833→    t_spk = time.time() - t0\n   834→    print(f'    Spike processing: {t_spk:.1f}s, {n_neurons} total units')\n   835→    \n   836→    if n_neurons < MIN_UNITS:\n   837→        print(f'    EXCLUDED: only {n_neurons} units (need >={MIN_UNITS})')\n   838→        return None\n   839→    \n   840→    # ---- Compute video offset ----\n   841→    vidshift = compute_video_offset(session)\n   842→    \n   843→    # ---- Extract tongue velocity ----\n   844→    t0 = time.time()\n   845→    tongue_vel = extract_feature_velocity(session, 0, 'tongue', time_axis, vidshift)\n   846→    t_tongue = time.time() - t0\n   847→    print(f'    Tongue velocity: {t_tongue:.1f}s')\n   848→    \n   849→    # ---- Extract paw velocity (top_paw from camera 1) ----\n   850→    t0 = time.time()\n   851→    paw_vel = extract_feature_velocity(session, 1, 'top_paw', time_axis, vidshift)\n   852→    t_paw = time.time() - t0\n   853→    print(f'    Paw velocity: {t_paw:.1f}s')\n   854→    \n   855→    # ---- Load and align motion energy ----\n   856→    t0 = time.time()\n   857→    if sess_info['me_filepath'] is not None:\n   858→        me_data = load_motion_energy(sess_info['me_filepath'])\n   859→        if me_data is not None:\n   860→            me_aligned = align_motion_energy(me_data, session, time_axis, vidshift)\n   861→        else:\n   862→            me_aligned = np.full((len(time_axis), session['bp']['Ntrials']), np.nan)\n   863→    else:\n   864→        me_aligned = np.full((len(time_axis), session['bp']['Ntrials']), np.nan)\n   865→    t_me = time.time() - t0\n   866→    print(f'    Motion energy: {t_me:.1f}s')\n   867→    \n   868→    # ---- Select valid trials ----\n   869→    # Include all trials that are not early and not stim\n   870→    bp = session['bp']\n   871→    valid_trials = ~bp['early'] & ~bp['stim_enable']\n   872→    # Also exclude 'no' (ignore) trials\n   873→    valid_trials = valid_trials & ~bp['no']\n   874→    \n   875→    trial_indices = np.where(valid_trials)[0]\n   876→    \n   877→    if len(trial_indices) < 2:\n   878→        print(f'    EXCLUDED: only {len(trial_indices)} valid trials')\n   879→        return None\n   880→    \n   881→    print(f'    Valid trials: {len(trial_indices)} / {bp[\"Ntrials\"]}')\n   882→    \n   883→    # ---- Compute per-session thresholds for discretization ----\n   884→    # Tongue velocity threshold: 50th percentile of all valid trial data\n   885→    tongue_valid = tongue_vel[:, trial_indices]\n   886→    tongue_thresh = np.nanpercentile(tongue_valid[~np.isnan(tongue_valid)], 50) if np.any(~np.isnan(tongue_valid)) else 0\n   887→    \n   888→    paw_valid = paw_vel[:, trial_indices]\n   889→    paw_thresh = np.nanpercentile(paw_valid[~np.isnan(paw_valid)], 50) if np.any(~np.isnan(paw_valid)) else 0\n   890→    \n   891→    me_valid = me_aligned[:, trial_indices]\n   892→    me_thresh = np.nanpercentile(me_valid[~np.isnan(me_valid)], 50) if np.any(~np.isnan(me_valid)) else 0\n   893→    \n   894→    print(f'    Thresholds: tongue={tongue_thresh:.2f}, paw={paw_thresh:.2f}, ME={me_thresh:.4f}')\n   895→    \n   896→    # ---- Build trial-level data ----\n   897→    neural_trials = []\n   898→    input_trials = []\n   899→    output_trials = []\n   900→    \n   901→    for trial_idx in trial_indices:\n   902→        # Neural: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n   903→        neural = firing_rates[:, :, trial_idx].astype(np.float32)\n   904→        neural_trials.append(neural)\n   905→        \n   906→        # Input: time from goCue (same for all trials)\n   907→        inp = time_axis.astype(np.float32).reshape(1, -1)  # (1, n_timepoints)\n   908→        input_trials.append(inp)\n   909→        \n   910→        # Output\n   911→        # Lick direction: R=1, L=0\n   912→        lick_dir = 1 if bp['R'][trial_idx] else 0\n   913→        \n   914→        # Behavioral context: WC=0, DR=1\n   915→        context = 0 if bp['autowater'][trial_idx] else 1\n   916→        \n   917→        # Outcome: correct=1, incorrect=0\n   918→        outcome = 1 if bp['hit'][trial_idx] else 0\n   919→        \n   920→        # Tongue velocity discretized (time-varying)\n   921→        tv = tongue_vel[:, trial_idx].copy()\n   922→        tv_disc = np.zeros(len(time_axis), dtype=np.int64)\n   923→        tv_disc[tv >= tongue_thresh] = 1\n   924→        # Handle NaN: set to 0 (below threshold)\n   925→        tv_disc[np.isnan(tv)] = 0\n   926→        \n   927→        # Paw velocity discretized (time-varying)\n   928→        pv = paw_vel[:, trial_idx].copy()\n   929→        pv_disc = np.zeros(len(time_axis), dtype=np.int64)\n   930→        pv_disc[pv >= paw_thresh] = 1\n   931→        pv_disc[np.isnan(pv)] = 0\n   932→        \n   933→        # Motion energy discretized (time-varying)\n   934→        me = me_aligned[:, trial_idx].copy()\n   935→        me_disc = np.zeros(len(time_axis), dtype=np.int64)\n   936→        me_disc[me >= me_thresh] = 1\n   937→        me_disc[np.isnan(me)] = 0\n   938→        \n   939→        # Stack outputs: (n_output, n_timepoints) for time-varying,\n   940→        # or (n_output,) for per-trial\n   941→        # Per-trial: lick_dir, context, outcome\n   942→        # Time-varying: tongue_vel, paw_vel, motion_energy\n   943→        out = np.zeros((6, len(time_axis)), dtype=np.int64)\n   944→        out[0, :] = lick_dir  # constant across time\n   945→        out[1, :] = context   # constant across time\n   946→        out[2, :] = outcome   # constant across time\n   947→        out[3, :] = tv_disc\n   948→        out[4, :] = pv_disc\n   949→        out[5, :] = me_disc\n   950→        \n   951→        output_trials.append(out)\n   952→    \n   953→    # ---- Visualization ----\n   954→    if show_processing:\n   955→        plot_processing(animal, date, session, firing_rates, tongue_vel, paw_vel,\n   956→                       me_aligned, time_axis, trial_indices, tongue_thresh, paw_thresh, me_thresh)\n   957→    \n   958→    result = {\n   959→        'neural': neural_trials,\n   960→        'input': input_trials,\n   961→        'output': output_trials,\n   962→        'animal': animal,\n   963→        'date': date,\n   964→        'n_neurons': n_neurons,\n   965→        'n_trials': len(trial_indices),\n   966→    }\n   967→    \n   968→    return result\n   969→\n   970→\n   971→def plot_processing(animal, date, session, firing_rates, tongue_vel, paw_vel,\n   972→                   me_aligned, time_axis, trial_indices, tongue_thresh, paw_thresh, me_thresh):\n   973→    \"\"\"Plot processing steps for visual verification.\"\"\"\n   974→    import matplotlib\n   975→    matplotlib.use('Agg')\n   976→    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt\n   977→    \n   978→    fig, axes = plt.subplots(4, 3, figsize=(20, 16))\n   979→    fig.suptitle(f'{animal}_{date}', fontsize=14)\n   980→    \n   981→    bp = session['bp']\n   982→    \n   983→    # Row 1: Neural activity examples\n   984→    n_neurons = firing_rates.shape[0]\n   985→    example_neurons = np.random.choice(n_neurons, min(3, n_neurons), replace=False)\n   986→    for col, neuron_idx in enumerate(example_neurons):\n   987→        ax = axes[0, col]\n   988→        # Plot a few trials\n   989→        n_show = min(10, len(trial_indices))\n   990→        for t in range(n_show):\n   991→            trix = trial_indices[t]\n   992→            ax.plot(time_axis, firing_rates[neuron_idx, :, trix], alpha=0.3)\n   993→        ax.axvline(0, color='k', linestyle='--', alpha=0.5, label='Go cue')\n   994→        ax.set_title(f'Neuron {neuron_idx}')\n   995→        ax.set_xlabel('Time from goCue (s)')\n   996→        ax.set_ylabel('Firing rate (Hz)')\n   997→    \n   998→    # Row 2: Tongue velocity\n   999→    ax = axes[1, 0]\n  1000→    for t in range(min(10, len(trial_indices))):\n  1001→        trix = trial_indices[t]\n  1002→        ax.plot(time_axis, tongue_vel[:, trix], alpha=0.3)\n  1003→    ax.axhline(tongue_thresh, color='r', linestyle='--', label=f'50th pct={tongue_thresh:.1f}')\n  1004→    ax.axvline(0, color='k', linestyle='--', alpha=0.5)\n  1005→    ax.set_title('Tongue velocity')\n  1006→    ax.legend()\n  1007→    \n  1008→    # Row 2: Paw velocity\n  1009→    ax = axes[1, 1]\n  1010→    for t in range(min(10, len(trial_indices))):\n  1011→        trix = trial_indices[t]\n  1012→        ax.plot(time_axis, paw_vel[:, trix], alpha=0.3)\n  1013→    ax.axhline(paw_thresh, color='r', linestyle='--', label=f'50th pct={paw_thresh:.1f}')\n  1014→    ax.axvline(0, color='k', linestyle='--', alpha=0.5)\n  1015→    ax.set_title('Paw velocity')\n  1016→    ax.legend()\n  1017→    \n  1018→    # Row 2: Motion energy\n  1019→    ax = axes[1, 2]\n  1020→    for t in range(min(10, len(trial_indices))):\n  1021→        trix = trial_indices[t]\n  1022→        ax.plot(time_axis, me_aligned[:, trix], alpha=0.3)\n  1023→    ax.axhline(me_thresh, color='r', linestyle='--', label=f'50th pct={me_thresh:.4f}')\n  1024→    ax.axvline(0, color='k', linestyle='--', alpha=0.5)\n  1025→    ax.set_title('Motion energy')\n  1026→    ax.legend()\n  1027→    \n  1028→    # Row 3: Discretized outputs example trial\n  1029→    ex_trix = trial_indices[0]\n  1030→    ax = axes[2, 0]\n  1031→    tv_disc = (tongue_vel[:, ex_trix] >= tongue_thresh).astype(int)\n  1032→    ax.plot(time_axis, tv_disc, 'b-', label='Tongue vel disc')\n  1033→    ax.axvline(0, color='k', linestyle='--', alpha=0.5)\n  1034→    ax.set_title(f'Discretized tongue vel (trial {ex_trix})')\n  1035→    ax.set_ylim(-0.1, 1.1)\n  1036→    \n  1037→    ax = axes[2, 1]\n  1038→    pv_disc = (paw_vel[:, ex_trix] >= paw_thresh).astype(int)\n  1039→    ax.plot(time_axis, pv_disc, 'g-', label='Paw vel disc')\n  1040→    ax.axvline(0, color='k', linestyle='--', alpha=0.5)\n  1041→    ax.set_title(f'Discretized paw vel (trial {ex_trix})')\n  1042→    ax.set_ylim(-0.1, 1.1)\n  1043→    \n  1044→    ax = axes[2, 2]\n  1045→    me_disc = (me_aligned[:, ex_trix] >= me_thresh).astype(int)\n  1046→    ax.plot(time_axis, me_disc, 'r-', label='ME disc')\n  1047→    ax.axvline(0, color='k', linestyle='--', alpha=0.5)\n  1048→    ax.set_title(f'Discretized ME (trial {ex_trix})')\n  1049→    ax.set_ylim(-0.1, 1.1)\n  1050→    \n  1051→    # Row 4: Trial outcome distributions\n  1052→    ax = axes[3, 0]\n  1053→    valid = trial_indices\n  1054→    r_count = np.sum(bp['R'][valid])\n  1055→    l_count = np.sum(bp['L'][valid])\n  1056→    ax.bar(['Right', 'Left'], [r_count, l_count])\n  1057→    ax.set_title('Lick direction')\n  1058→    \n  1059→    ax = axes[3, 1]\n  1060→    dr_count = np.sum(~bp['autowater'][valid])\n  1061→    wc_count = np.sum(bp['autowater'][valid])\n  1062→    ax.bar(['DR', 'WC'], [dr_count, wc_count])\n  1063→    ax.set_title('Context')\n  1064→    \n  1065→    ax = axes[3, 2]\n  1066→    hit_count = np.sum(bp['hit'][valid])\n  1067→    miss_count = np.sum(bp['miss'][valid])\n  1068→    ax.bar(['Hit', 'Miss'], [hit_count, miss_count])\n  1069→    ax.set_title('Outcome')\n  1070→    \n  1071→    plt.tight_layout()\n  1072→    plt.savefig(f'processing_{animal}_{date}.png', dpi=100)\n  1073→    plt.close()\n  1074→    print(f'    Saved processing_{animal}_{date}.png')\n  1075→\n  1076→\n  1077→def main():\n  1078→    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description='Convert Hasnain et al. 2024 data')\n  1079→    parser.add_argument('output', help='Output pickle file path')\n  1080→    parser.add_argument('--sample', action='store_true', help='Process only 2 sessions')\n  1081→    parser.add_argument('--full', action='store_true', help='Process all sessions (default)')\n  1082→    parser.add_argument('--show-processing', action='store_true', help='Plot processing visualizations')\n  1083→    args = parser.parse_args()\n  1084→    \n  1085→    total_start = time.time()\n  1086→    \n  1087→    # Get available sessions\n  1088→    all_sessions = get_available_sessions()\n  1089→    print(f'Found {len(all_sessions)} total sessions')\n  1090→    \n  1091→    if args.sample:\n  1092→        # Select 2 sessions for testing\n  1093→        all_sessions = all_sessions[:2]\n  1094→        print(f'Sample mode: processing {len(all_sessions)} sessions')\n  1095→    \n  1096→    # Create time axis\n  1097→    time_axis = make_time_axis()\n  1098→    print(f'Time axis: {len(time_axis)} bins, [{time_axis[0]:.3f}, {time_axis[-1]:.3f}] s')\n  1099→    \n  1100→    # Process all sessions\n  1101→    results = []\n  1102→    for i, sess_info in enumerate(all_sessions):\n  1103→        print(f'\\nSession {i+1}/{len(all_sessions)}: {sess_info[\"animal\"]}_{sess_info[\"date\"]}')\n  1104→        t0 = time.time()\n  1105→        \n  1106→        try:\n  1107→            result = process_session(sess_info, time_axis, show_processing=args.show_processing)\n  1108→            if result is not None:\n  1109→                results.append(result)\n  1110→                print(f'  -> Included: {result[\"n_neurons\"]} neurons, {result[\"n_trials\"]} trials')\n  1111→            else:\n  1112→                print(f'  -> Excluded')\n  1113→        except Exception as e:\n  1114→            print(f'  -> ERROR: {e}')\n  1115→            import traceback\n  1116→            traceback.print_exc()\n  1117→        \n  1118→        elapsed = time.time() - t0\n  1119→        print(f'  Session time: {elapsed:.1f}s')\n  1120→    \n  1121→    print(f'\\n{len(results)} sessions included')\n  1122→    \n  1123→    if len(results) == 0:\n  1124→        print('ERROR: No sessions passed inclusion criteria!')\n  1125→        sys.exit(1)\n  1126→    \n  1127→    # ---- Assemble final data structure ----\n  1128→    print('\\nAssembling final data structure...')\n  1129→    \n  1130→    # Collect all unique subjects\n  1131→    subjects = sorted(set(r['animal'] for r in results))\n  1132→    subject_idx = np.array([subjects.index(r['animal']) for r in results])\n  1133→    \n  1134→    # Brain regions - all ALM\n  1135→    brain_regions = ['ALM']\n  1136→    brain_region_idx = [np.zeros(r['n_neurons'], dtype=np.int64) for r in results]\n  1137→    \n  1138→    # Build lists\n  1139→    neural = [r['neural'] for r in results]\n  1140→    inputs = [r['input'] for r in results]\n  1141→    outputs = [r['output'] for r in results]\n  1142→    \n  1143→    data = {\n  1144→        'neural': neural,\n  1145→        'input': inputs,\n  1146→        'output': outputs,\n  1147→        'subjects': subjects,\n  1148→        'subject_idx': subject_idx,\n  1149→        'brain_regions': brain_regions,\n  1150→        'brain_region_idx': brain_region_idx,\n  1151→        'input_names': ['time_from_go_cue'],\n  1152→        'output_names': ['lick_direction', 'behavioral_context', 'outcome',\n  1153→                        'tongue_velocity', 'paw_velocity', 'motion_energy'],\n  1154→        'output_values': [\n  1155→            ['left', 'right'],           # lick_direction: 0=left, 1=right\n  1156→            ['WC', 'DR'],                # behavioral_context: 0=WC, 1=DR\n  1157→            ['incorrect', 'correct'],    # outcome: 0=incorrect, 1=correct\n  1158→            ['low', 'high'],             # tongue_velocity: 0=<50th, 1=>=50th\n  1159→            ['low', 'high'],             # paw_velocity: 0=<50th, 1=>=50th\n  1160→            ['low', 'high'],             # motion_energy: 0=<50th, 1=>=50th\n  1161→        ],\n  1162→        'metadata': {\n  1163→            'task_description': 'Two-context paradigm: delayed-response (DR) and water-cued (WC) licking tasks alternating block-wise. Mice perform directional tongue movements to left or right targets.',\n  1164→            'time_bin_size': DT * 1000,  # 5 ms\n  1165→            'temporal_alignment_event': 'Go cue onset',\n  1166→            'off_start': TMIN,  # -2.5 seconds\n  1167→            'off_end': TMAX,    # 2.5 seconds\n  1168→            'smoothing': f'Causal Gaussian, window={SMOOTH_N} bins ({SMOOTH_N * DT * 1000:.0f} ms)',\n  1169→            'firing_rate_threshold': LOW_FR,\n  1170→            'brain_region': 'Anterior Lateral Motor cortex (ALM)',\n  1171→            'species': 'Mus musculus',\n  1172→            'reference': 'Hasnain, Birnbaum et al, Nature Neuroscience 2024',\n  1173→            'session_info': [{'animal': r['animal'], 'date': r['date'],\n  1174→                            'n_neurons': r['n_neurons'], 'n_trials': r['n_trials']}\n  1175→                           for r in results],\n  1176→        }\n  1177→    }\n  1178→    \n  1179→    # Print summary\n  1180→    total_neurons = sum(r['n_neurons'] for r in results)\n  1181→    total_trials = sum(r['n_trials'] for r in results)\n  1182→    print(f'\\nSummary:')\n  1183→    print(f'  Sessions: {len(results)}')\n  1184→    print(f'  Subjects: {len(subjects)}')\n  1185→    print(f'  Total neurons: {total_neurons}')\n  1186→    print(f'  Total trials: {total_trials}')\n  1187→    print(f'  Mean neurons/session: {total_neurons/len(results):.1f}')\n  1188→    print(f'  Mean trials/session: {total_trials/len(results):.1f}')\n  1189→    print(f'  Time bins per trial: {len(time_axis)}')\n  1190→    \n  1191→    # Filter out garbage clusters\n    valid_units = []\n    for u_idx, unit in enumerate(units):\n        quality = unit['quality'].lower().strip()\n        if 'garbage' not in quality:\n            valid_units.append(u_idx)\n    \n    if len(valid_units) == 0:\n        return None, []\n    \n    # Compute firing rates for valid units\n    firing_rates = np.zeros((len(valid_units), n_time, Ntrials), dtype=np.float32)\n    \n    for i, u_idx in enumerate(valid_units):\n        unit = units[u_idx]\n        trial_nums = unit['trial']\n        trialtm = unit['trialtm']\n        \n        for j in range(Ntrials):\n            trial_num = j + 1  # MATLAB 1-indexed\n            spk_mask = trial_nums == trial_num\n            \n            if not np.any(spk_mask):\n                continue\n            \n            # Align to goCue\n            aligned_times = trialtm[spk_mask] - goCue[j]\n            \n            # Bin spikes\n            counts, _ = np.histogram(aligned_times, bins=edges)\n            \n            # Convert to firing rate and smooth\n            fr = counts.astype(np.float64) / DT\n            fr_smooth = smooth_signal(fr, SMOOTH_N)\n            firing_rates[i, :, j] = fr_smooth\n    \n    #
